for the exchange 4-point function can be extracted from our knowledge of the scheme-dependent terms for all relevant 3-point functions. We arrive at:

Fact 4

The piece
i^{\rm div}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}
containing all divergences and all finite scheme-dependent terms for the exchange 4-point amplitudes with
d=3
and
\Delta_{j}=2
or
3
can be computed as

\displaystyle s^{2\hat{\beta}_{x}}i^{\rm div}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}=\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)\,\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}\Delta_{x}]}(k_{3},k_{4},s)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=0\\
L_{0}\neq 0\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\mathcal{I}_{(\sigma_{j},n)}(k_{3},k_{4},s)}{L(\sigma_{j},n)}\,j^{\rm div}_{-\hat{d}-1+L,[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1\\
\{L_{0}=0\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathcal{I}_{{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}}(k_{3},k_{4},s)}{\epsilon\,L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}}\,\hat{j}_{-\hat{d}-1+\epsilon\,L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}},[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}=\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=0\\
L^{\prime}_{0}\neq 0\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\mathcal{J}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)}(k_{3},k_{4},s)}{L^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)}\,i^{\rm div}_{-\hat{d}-1+L^{\prime},[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{x}]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}=\pm 1\\
\{L^{\prime}_{0}=0\}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathcal{J}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(k_{3},k_{4},s)}{\epsilon\,L^{\prime}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}}\,\hat{i}_{-\hat{d}-1+\epsilon\,L^{\prime}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}},[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{x}]}(k_{1},k_{2},s),

(268)

where
\mathcal{I}_{{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}}(k_{3},k_{4},s)
is defined in (
231
) and
\mathcal{J}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)}(k_{3},k_{4},s)
has an analogous definition. The summation in the second and fourth terms is over all signs and all integers such that
L_{0}
and
L_{0}^{\prime}
are not equal to zero, respectively. These summations are finite, since we only need to consider the amplitudes with

\displaystyle L\leq\hat{\beta}_{1}+\hat{\beta}_{2}-\hat{\beta}_{x}-\frac{\hat{d}}{2},

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\leq\hat{\beta}_{1}+\hat{\beta}_{2}+\hat{\beta}_{x}-\frac{\hat{d}}{2}.

(269)

The summations in the third and fifth terms are over the locus of
L_{0}=0
and
L_{0}^{\prime}=0
, respectively.

Thus, if we already know the amplitude
\hat{i}(\bar{u},\bar{v}_{j})
regulated in
some
scheme with parameters
\bar{u}
and
\bar{v}_{j}
, we can use it to obtain the amplitude in
any
regularization scheme,

\displaystyle\hat{i}(u,v_{j})=\hat{i}(\bar{u},\bar{v}_{j})+\left[i^{\rm div}(u,v_{j})-i^{\rm div}(\bar{u},\bar{v}_{j})\right]+O(\epsilon).

(270)

The bound (
269
) reflects the fact that for
L
and
L^{\prime}
sufficiently large, the amplitudes in (
5.4.2
) become finite and thus scheme-independent. To figure out how large
L
and
L^{\prime}
need to be for the amplitudes to be finite, we power expand the integrands of
j_{-d-1+L,[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}
and
i_{-d-1+L^{\prime},[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{x}]}
. The lowest powers of the integration variables in the two cases are
\tfrac{1}{2}d-1+L-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}+\beta_{3}
for the
j
amplitude and
\tfrac{1}{2}d-1+L^{\prime}-\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}-\beta_{3}
for
i
. The amplitudes are finite if these combinations are strictly greater than
-1
. Equivalently, divergent amplitudes can only occur for
L
and
L^{\prime}
satisfying (
269
).

In order to use this fact we must be careful to evaluate (
4
) including all finite terms of order
\epsilon^{0}
. This means that one generally needs to evaluate all 3-point amplitudes up to and including terms of order
\epsilon^{1}
. For example, in the first line both 3-point amplitudes may be linearly divergent. Thus, in order to extract correctly the finite
u
and
v_{j}
-dependent terms we have to multiply the divergence of one amplitude by terms of order
\epsilon^{1}
in the other. This is the reason why we computed the order
\epsilon
parameter-dependent terms in 3-point amplitudes in Fact
2
.

Example

Let us discuss an example of the scheme change for the amplitude
\hat{i}_{[33,22x2]}
. To make the expressions slightly shorter, let us use only two parameters
v_{[2]}
and
v_{[3]}
to regulate the corresponding dimensions,
i.e.
, for
\Delta=2
the regulated dimension is
\hat{\Delta}=2+(u+v_{[2]})\epsilon
, while for
\Delta=3
we take
\hat{\Delta}=3+(u+v_{[3]})\epsilon
.

First, we find out which 3-point amplitudes are needed in (
4
). The first term is the product of
\hat{i}_{[222]}
and
\hat{j}_{[33;2]}
. Since the amplitude
\hat{j}_{[33;2]}
is finite (see Table
4
), it is enough to know
\hat{i}_{[222]}
only up to finite order. On the other hand, we need all
u
and
v_{j}
-dependent terms, including terms of order
\epsilon
in
\hat{j}_{[33;2]}
as they get multiplied by the divergence of
\hat{i}_{[222]}
.

For the next two terms we must power expand the integrands of
\hat{i}_{[222]}
and
\hat{j}_{[22;2]}
. We must include all terms of order
z^{L-1}
with
L
and
L^{\prime}
bounded by (
269
), which in this case evaluates to
L\leq 1
and
L^{\prime}\leq 2
. Power expanding the integrands of
\hat{j}_{-4-2u\epsilon+L,[22;2]}
and
\hat{i}_{-4-2u\epsilon+L^{\prime},[222]}
we find the divergent pieces
j^{\text{div}}_{-4-2u\epsilon+L,[22;2]}
and
i^{\text{div}}_{-4-2u\epsilon+L^{\prime},[222]}
in (
4
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{-1+\epsilon(u-3v_{[2]})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\zeta^{\epsilon(u-v_{[2]})}\frac{\Gamma(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}\left(k_{3}^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}+k_{4}^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}+s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+O(\zeta),

(271)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{\epsilon(u-v_{[2]})}\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2\Gamma(\tfrac{3}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\zeta^{1+\epsilon(u+v_{[2]})}\frac{\Gamma(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2^{2+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\Gamma(\tfrac{3}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}\left[(k_{3}s)^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}+(k_{4}s)^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+O(\zeta^{2}).

(272)

When integrated term-by-term, the first term in
\mathcal{I}
produces a singularity at
\epsilon=0
. This term corresponds to the third line of (
4
). Indeed, the condition
L_{0}=0
, with
L_{0}
defined in (
228
) has a unique solution corresponding to all
\sigma_{j}=-1
and
n(\sigma_{j})=0
as
\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}=\beta_{x}=1/2
. Similarly, the fact that the expansion (
272
) does not contain terms of order
-1+O(\epsilon)
indicates that the last line of (
4
) is absent. Equivalently,
L^{\prime}_{0}
defined in (
266
) does not vanish for any combination of signs. This means that all remaining terms in (
5.4.3
) and (
272
) give rise to the scheme-dependent terms in the second and fourth line of (
4
). In total, we find

\displaystyle s^{1+2v_{[2]}\epsilon}i^{\text{div}}_{[33,22x2]}

\displaystyle=\hat{j}_{[33;2]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)\hat{i}_{[222]}(k_{3},k_{4},s)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{\epsilon(u-3v_{[2]})}\times\hat{j}_{-4-\epsilon(u+3v_{[2]}),[33;2]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\Gamma(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}(1+\epsilon(u-v_{[2]}))\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}\left(k_{3}^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}+k_{4}^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}+s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\right)\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times j^{\text{div}}_{-3-\epsilon(u+v_{[2]}),[33;2]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2(1+\epsilon(u-v_{[2]}))\Gamma(\tfrac{3}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}i^{\text{div}}_{-3-\epsilon(u+v_{[2]}),[332]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\Gamma(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2^{2+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}(2+\epsilon(u+v_{[2]}))\Gamma(\tfrac{3}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}\left[(k_{3}s)^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}+(k_{4}s)^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\right]\times

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times i^{\text{div}}_{-2-\epsilon(u-v_{[2]}),[332]}(k_{1},k_{2},s).

(273)

To complete the calculation, we must be able to evaluate the amplitudes on the right-hand side. The divergent terms can always be evaluated by means of the definition (
232
). For example, to calculate
j^{\text{div}}_{-3-\epsilon(u+v_{[2]}),[33;2]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)
we power expand the integrand in (
261
) and pick the term of order
\zeta^{-1+O(\epsilon)}
. To be precise, the power expansion reads

\displaystyle\zeta^{-3-\epsilon(u+v_{[2]})}\hat{\K}_{[3]}(\zeta,k_{1})\hat{\K}_{[3]}(\zeta,k_{2})\hat{\II}_{[2]}(\zeta,s)=\frac{s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2\Gamma(\tfrac{3}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}\zeta^{-1+2\epsilon(u-v_{[3]})}+O(\zeta^{0}).

(274)

Thus,

\displaystyle j^{\text{div}}_{-3-\epsilon(u+v_{[2]}),[33;2]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle=\frac{s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2\Gamma(\tfrac{3}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}\int_{0}^{\mu^{-1}}\mathrm{d}\zeta\,\zeta^{-1+2\epsilon(u-v_{[3]})}

\displaystyle=\frac{s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2\Gamma(\tfrac{3}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})}\times\frac{\mu^{-2\epsilon(u-v_{[3]})}}{2\epsilon\,(u-v_{[3]})}.

(275)

In the same fashion we find

\displaystyle i^{\text{div}}_{-3-\epsilon(u+v_{[2]}),[332]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle=\frac{s^{1+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\mu^{-2\epsilon(u-v_{[3]})}\Gamma(-\tfrac{1}{2}-\epsilon v_{[2]})}{2^{2+2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\epsilon\,(u-v_{[3]})\Gamma(\tfrac{1}{2}+\epsilon v_{[2]})},

(276)

\displaystyle i^{\text{div}}_{-2-\epsilon(u-v_{[2]}),[332]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{-2\epsilon(u-v_{[3]})}}{2\epsilon\,(u-v_{[3]})}.

(277)

The remaining term can be evaluated including terms of order
\epsilon^{1}
, since
\hat{j}_{-4-\epsilon(u+3v_{[2]}),[33;2]}
equals
\hat{j}_{[33;2]}
evaluated in the scheme with the
u
-parameter equal to
2u-3v_{[2]}
,
i.e.
,

\displaystyle\hat{j}_{-4-\epsilon(u+3v_{[2]}),[33;2]}(u;v_{[2]},v_{[3]})=\hat{j}_{[33;2]}(2u-3v_{[2]};v_{[2]},v_{[3]}).

(278)

When these results are substituted back to (
5.4.3
) and power expanded we arrive at

\displaystyle i^{\text{div}}_{[33,22x2]}

\displaystyle=s^{-1-2\epsilon v_{[2]}}\left[\frac{\hat{j}^{2u-3v_{[2]}\{v_{[3]}v_{[3]}v_{[2]}\}}_{[33;2]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)}{\epsilon\,(-u+3v_{[2]})}+\hat{j}_{[33;2]}(k_{1},k_{2},s)\hat{i}_{[222]}(s,k_{3},k_{4})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{k_{3}}{2(u-v_{[3]})}\left[\frac{1}{2\epsilon}+v_{[2]}(\log k_{3}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-\tfrac{7}{4})+\frac{1}{4}(-3u+4v_{[2]})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{k_{4}}{2(u-v_{[3]})}\left[\frac{1}{2\epsilon}+v_{[2]}(\log k_{4}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-\tfrac{7}{4})+\frac{1}{4}(-3u+4v_{[2]})\right]+O(\epsilon).

(279)

In the first term we used the convention where, in the superscript, we indicate the value of the
u
and
v_{j}
parameters at which the 3-point amplitude should be evaluated,

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{u\{v_{1}v_{2}v_{3}\}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})

\displaystyle=\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3};\hat{d}=3+2u\epsilon,\hat{\Delta}_{j}=\Delta_{j}+(u+v_{j})\epsilon),

(280)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{u\{v_{1}v_{2}v_{3}\}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})

\displaystyle=\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3};\hat{d}=3+2u\epsilon,\hat{\Delta}_{j}=\Delta_{j}+(u+v_{j})\epsilon).

(281)

All results are listed in Section
5.5
. There, we list first the 3-point amplitudes evaluated to order
\epsilon^{1}
, then the regulated 4-point amplitudes evaluated in an arbitrary scheme.

Proofs

Our goal in this subsection is to prove Fact
2
and Fact
4
. The starting point is the analysis of singularities of the regulated 3-point amplitude (
7
). The full analysis is carried out in
Bzowski:2015yxv
, but here we will limit our attention to the cases at hand,
i.e.
, we assume unregulated
\beta_{j}
parameters that are non-integral. This implies that all expansion coefficients in (
501
) are continuous at
\epsilon=0
, meaning one can split the integral in (
7
) into two regions. Choosing
\mu>0
, we write

\displaystyle\hat{i}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mu^{-1}}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}z\,\mathcal{I}+\int_{0}^{\mu^{-1}}\mathrm{d}z\sum_{L}\mathcal{I}_{L}z^{L-1}.

(282)

Due to the exponential fall-off of the Bessel functions, the first integral converges for all
\alpha
and
\beta_{j}
. By the dominated convergence theorem, one can pass the
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
limit through the integral. For the second integral, recall that the regulated amplitude
\hat{i}
is defined by the analytic continuation of the parameters
\alpha
and
\beta_{j}
to the region of convergence. In other words, we can assume that the powers of
z
are all greater than
-1
and at least the integral of each term separately exists. Thus, to commute the sum and the integral it is enough to show that the series converges to an integrable function when the
\mathcal{I}_{L}
are replaced by their absolute values,
|\mathcal{I}_{L}|
. This is indeed the case, since almost all coefficients
a_{n}(\nu)
are positive,
i.e.
,
a_{n}(\nu)>0
for all
n\geq\nu
. Since the radius of convergence of (
500
) is infinite, we can reverse the order of summation and integration in (
282
) by the Fubini’s theorem. This shows the following:

Fact 5

The amplitude
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}
is divergent at
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
if and only if there exist independent choices of signs
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1
and a non-negative integer
n
such that
L_{0}(\sigma_{j},n)=0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{2}+\sigma_{1}\beta_{1}+\sigma_{2}\beta_{2}+\sigma_{3}\beta_{3}=-2n.

(283)

The same argument holds for the amplitudes
\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}
, except that the corresponding condition is

\displaystyle L^{\prime}_{0}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)=L_{0}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},+1,n)=0.

(284)

Since the sign of
\sigma_{3}
is fixed to
+1
, the amplitudes
\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}
cannot be more singular than
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}
. The following table presents the rank of the divergence at
\epsilon=0
of the 3-point amplitudes:

Dimensions

\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}

\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}

[22;2]

1

0

[22;3]

1

0

[32;2]

1

1

[32;3]

1

0

[33;2]

1

0

[33;3]

1

1

Table 4:
Degrees of singularities of 3-point amplitudes.

The reasoning above is sufficient to prove Fact
1
. Indeed, as we can see, all terms in (
282
) have a finite
\epsilon=0
limit except the ones connected with
L_{0}=0
. Thus, all parameter-dependent terms are contained in
i^{\text{div}}
. This is sufficient to prove the first two equations in Fact
2
. However, since we also want to derive the scheme-dependent terms in terms of order
\epsilon^{1}
, we must first prove the following:

Fact 6

Consider a regulated amplitude
\hat{i}(u,v_{j})
exhibiting a first order pole as
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
. As far as the
u
and
v_{j}
-dependence of the amplitude is concerned, one has the decomposition

\displaystyle i^{\rm div}(u,v_{j})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon}\,\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\frac{A_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}}{L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+B+\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{B_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}^{(j)}v_{j}}{L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\epsilon\left[Cu+\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}C_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}^{(j)}v_{j}+\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}\frac{C^{(ij)}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}v_{i}v_{j}}{L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}}\right]+O(\epsilon^{2}),

(285)

where
L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
is given by (
228
) and
A_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}},B,B_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}^{(j)},C,C_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}^{(j)},C^{(ij)}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
are all
u
- and
v_{j}
-independent. The same structure holds for
\hat{i}(u,v_{j})
.

This fact follows from the structure of terms in (
282
). All terms in (
282
), except (
232
), are finite as
\epsilon\to 0
.
The upper integral in (
282
) is finite and scheme-independent. The finite terms of the lower integral are given by

i^{\rm finite}=\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=0\\
L_{0}\neq 0\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\mathcal{I}_{(\sigma_{j},n)}\mu^{-L(\sigma_{j},n)}}{L(\sigma_{j},n)}.

(286)

As shown in (
229
),

\mathcal{I}_{(\sigma_{j},n)}
is finite and scheme-independent as
\epsilon\to 0
, and the order
\epsilon
term is linear in
v_{j}
but independent of
u
(more generally, the
n
th order term in their Taylor expansion around
\epsilon=0

is a polynomial of order
n
in
v_{j}
and has no
u
dependence). Similarly, the
\epsilon,u,v_{j}
dependence of terms depending on
L(\sigma_{j},n)
follow from (
227
)-(
228
). When
L_{0}\neq 0
, such terms are finite and scheme-independent as
\epsilon\to 0
, and the
n
th order term in their Taylor expansion around
\epsilon=0

is a polynomial of order
n
in
u,v_{j}
. It follows that in the absence of divergences the
\epsilon
-expansion of any amplitude takes the form (
6
) with
A_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}=B_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}^{(j)}=C^{(ij)}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}=0
.

Divergent terms appear only from (
232
) and have the form presented. Expanding these in
\epsilon
, we see that we can obtain a finite scheme-dependent term from the order
\epsilon
part of
\mathcal{I}_{(\sigma_{j},n)}
, which thus depends only on
v_{j}
, and their expansion to order
\epsilon
yields terms that linear in
u
and quadratic in
v_{j}
. Thus (
6
) contains all divergent and all scheme-dependent terms through order
\epsilon
.

Using the above fact we can derive Fact
2
. Consider the finite terms first. Notice that
B
is scheme-independent and the remaining term in the second line vanishes at
v_{j}=0
. Thus
B
is the finite part of
\hat{i}(1,0)
evaluated in the half-integer scheme. To recover
B^{(j)}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
we use (
233
) with
\bar{u}=1
and
\bar{v}_{j}=0
, which tells us that

\displaystyle\left[\hat{i}(u,v_{j})-\hat{i}(1,0)\right]_{\epsilon^{0}}=\left[i^{\text{div}}(u,v_{j})-i^{\text{div}}(1,0)\right]_{\epsilon^{0}}.

(287)

Since only
i^{\text{div}}(u,v_{j})
is
v_{j}
-dependent on the right hand side, we can find
B^{(j)}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
by retrieving the coefficient of
v_{j}/L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
in
i^{\text{div}}(u,v_{j})
. Indeed, using (
229
) we see that
i^{\text{div}}
contains precise a terms of this form. In conclusion,

\displaystyle\hat{i}(u,v_{j})|_{\epsilon^{0}}=\hat{i}(1,0)|_{\epsilon^{0}}+\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}i^{\text{div}}(u,v_{j};1)|_{\epsilon^{0}}.

(288)

An analogous argument holds for terms of order
\epsilon^{1}
. Since for
v_{j}=0
only the first term
C
survives, its value is equal to the term of order
\epsilon
in
\hat{i}(1,0)
. The term linear in
v_{j}
is similarly determined by the derivative
\partial\hat{i}/\partial\beta_{j}
, and the last term, with
L_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
in the denominator, is accounted for by
i^{\text{div}}
.
We simply power expand (
232
) to order
\epsilon
and keep terms quadratic in the
v_{j}
. This determines
C^{(ij)}_{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}
. All in all, we have found a simple prescription to calculate any 3-point amplitude
\hat{i}(u,v_{j})
to order
\epsilon^{1}
in any regularization scheme.

Let us now discuss Fact
4
. To prove Fact
4
, it suffices to show that
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}-i^{\text{div}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}
is finite and scheme-independent. The difference is equal to

\displaystyle s^{2\hat{\beta}_{x}}\left(\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}-i^{\rm div}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}\right)=

(289)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=0\\
L_{0}\neq 0\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\mathcal{I}_{(\sigma_{j},n)}(k_{3},k_{4},s)}{L(\sigma_{j},n)}\,\left(\hat{j}_{-\hat{d}-1+L,[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}-j^{\rm div}_{-\hat{d}-1+L,[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}=\pm 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n=0\\
L^{\prime}_{0}\neq 0\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{\mathcal{J}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)}(k_{3},k_{4},s)}{L^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)}\,\left(\hat{i}_{-\hat{d}-1+L^{\prime},[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}-i^{\rm div}_{-\hat{d}-1+L^{\prime},[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{x}]}\right)

(290)

By construction,
(\hat{j}-j^{\rm div})
and
(\hat{i}-i^{\text{div}})
are finite and scheme-independent, and we also showed earlier that

\mathcal{I}_{(\sigma_{j},n)},L(\sigma_{j},n),\mathcal{J}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)},L^{\prime}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},n)
are finite and scheme-independent as
\epsilon\to 0
. It follows that the right hand side of (
289
) is indeed finite and scheme-independent.

List of results

In this section we list:

•

All 3-point amplitudes
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}(u,v_{j})
and
\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}(u,v_{j})
for
\Delta_{j}=2
or
3
in an arbitrary regularization scheme, up to and including terms of order
\epsilon^{1}
.

•

All 4-point amplitudes
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}]}(u,v_{j})
and
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(u,v_{j})
regulated in an arbitrary regularization scheme.

To do so, we introduce the following definitions.

Definitions

•

The amplitudes
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}
and
\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}
are defined in (
7
) and (
257
). We consider the general regularization scheme (
2
).

•

3-point amplitudes evaluated up to and including order
\epsilon^{1}
are decomposed as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}

\displaystyle=i^{\text{div}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}+i^{\text{fin}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}+\epsilon\left[u\,\hat{i}^{u\epsilon}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}v_{j}\hat{i}^{v_{j}\epsilon}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}\right]+O(\epsilon^{2}),

(291)

\displaystyle\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}

\displaystyle=j^{\text{div}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}+j^{\text{fin}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}+\epsilon\left[u\,\hat{j}^{u\epsilon}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}+\sum_{j=1}^{3}v_{j}\hat{j}^{v_{j}\epsilon}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}\right]+O(\epsilon^{2}),

(292)

where

\displaystyle i^{\text{div}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}=\pm 1}i^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]},

(293)

\displaystyle j^{\text{div}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}=\pm 1}j^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}+}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}.

(294)

Only the non-vanishing terms
i^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}
and
j^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}+}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}
are listed.

•

The divergences of the amplitude are contained entirely in the divergent parts
i^{\text{div}}
and
j^{\text{div}}
. All remaining terms are parameter-independent.

•

3-point amplitudes are treated as functions of the momentum magnitudes
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}
. We also use the notation

\displaystyle k_{t}

\displaystyle=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},

\displaystyle v_{t}

\displaystyle=v_{1}+v_{2}+v_{3},

\displaystyle l_{12-}

\displaystyle=k_{1}+k_{2}-k_{3},

(295)

and analogously for
l_{13-}
and
l_{23-}
. This follows from (
482
) as
|\bm{k}_{1}+\bm{k}_{2}|=k_{3}
.

•

For 4-point functions, we use the notations

\displaystyle k_{T}

\displaystyle=k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4},

\displaystyle v_{T}

\displaystyle=v_{1}+v_{2}+v_{3}+v_{4}.

(296)

•

An exchange 4-point function
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(u,v_{1},v_{2},v_{3},v_{4},v_{x})
can be evaluated in the regularization scheme (
2
) by means of the formula

\displaystyle\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(u,v_{j})

\displaystyle=\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(1,0)

\displaystyle\quad+\left[i^{\text{div}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(u,v_{j})-i^{\text{div}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(1,0)\right].

(297)

The amplitudes
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2},\Delta_{3}\Delta_{4}x\Delta_{x}]}(1,0)
are evaluated in the half-integer scheme and they are listed in Section
3
. The divergent terms are then listed in Section
5.5.5
.

•

The divergent terms for the 4-point amplitudes contain 3-point amplitudes evaluated in some specific schemes. To avoid clutter, we use the following notation,

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{u\{v_{1}v_{2}v_{3}\}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})

\displaystyle=\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3};\hat{d}=3+2u\epsilon,\hat{\Delta}_{j}=\Delta_{j}+(u+v_{j})\epsilon),

(298)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{u\{v_{1}v_{2}v_{3}\}}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})

\displaystyle=\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3};\hat{d}=3+2u\epsilon,\hat{\Delta}_{j}=\Delta_{j}+(u+v_{j})\epsilon),

(299)

where the subscripts indicate the values of the
u
and
v_{j}
-parameters.

•

Certain expressions are long and unwieldy – despite our best efforts to avoid typos, when in doubt, we recommend using the results stored in the Mathematica notebooks.

3-point amplitudes
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}

The expansions of the amplitudes
\hat{i}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2}\Delta_{3}]}
to order
\epsilon^{1}
are as follows:

•

For
\hat{i}_{[222]}
:

\displaystyle i^{---}_{[222]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\epsilon\,(u-v_{t})},

(300)

\displaystyle i^{\text{fin}}_{[222]}

\displaystyle=-\log k_{t}-\gamma_{E},

(301)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{u\epsilon}_{[222]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E})^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{12},

(302)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[222]}

\displaystyle=-\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{23-}}{k_{t}}\right)-\frac{1}{2}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E})^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{12},

(303)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[222]}

\displaystyle=\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[222]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2}),

(304)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{3}\epsilon}_{[222]}

\displaystyle=\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[222]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{3}).

(305)

•

For
\hat{i}_{[322]}
:

\displaystyle i^{-+-}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{2}}{u-v_{1}+v_{2}-v_{3}}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon}-2v_{2}(\log k_{2}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-1)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-2v_{2}^{2}\epsilon\left((\log k_{2}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-1)^{2}+1\right)\right],

(306)

\displaystyle i^{--+}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=i^{-+-}_{[322]}(k_{2}\leftrightarrow k_{3},v_{2}\leftrightarrow v_{3}),

(307)

\displaystyle i^{\text{fin}}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=(k_{2}+k_{3})(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)-k_{1},

(308)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{u\epsilon}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{2}\left[(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}+1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right]+k_{1}\left(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1\right),

(309)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{2}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{23-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}+1-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+k_{1}\left(\log k_{t}-2\log k_{1}-\gamma_{E}-2\log 2+3\right),

(310)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{3}-k_{2}}{2}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{13-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}+1-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+2k_{2}\left[(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)(\log k_{2}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-1)+1\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-k_{1}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1),

(311)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{3}\epsilon}_{[322]}

\displaystyle=\hat{i}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[322]}(k_{2}\leftrightarrow k_{3}).

(312)

•

For
\hat{i}_{[332]}
:

\displaystyle i^{---}_{[332]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{u-v_{t}}\left[\frac{k_{3}^{2}-k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2}}{2\epsilon}+(v_{1}k_{1}^{2}+v_{2}k_{2}^{2}+v_{3}k_{3}^{2})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-2\epsilon(v_{1}^{2}k_{1}^{2}+v_{2}^{2}k_{2}^{2}-v_{3}^{2}k_{3}^{2})\right],

(313)

\displaystyle i^{\text{fin}}_{[332]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-k_{3}^{2})\left(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}\right)-\frac{1}{4}k_{t}(k_{1}+k_{2}-3k_{3}),

(314)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{u\epsilon}_{[332]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-k_{3}^{2}}{4}\left[(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})(k_{1}k_{2}-k_{1}k_{3}-k_{2}k_{3}-k_{3}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{k_{3}(k_{1}+k_{2})}{2}-\frac{k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-9k_{3}^{2}}{16},

(315)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[332]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-k_{3}^{2}}{4}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{23-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+k_{1}(k_{3}-k_{2})(\log k_{1}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}(k_{1}+k_{3})(2k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}(3k_{1}k_{2}-4k_{1}k_{3}-k_{2}k_{3}-k_{3}^{2}),

(316)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[332]}

\displaystyle=\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[332]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2}),

(317)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{3}\epsilon}_{[332]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-k_{3}^{2}}{4}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}+\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}(k_{1}+k_{3})(k_{2}+k_{3})(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+(k_{1}+k_{2})k_{3}(\log k_{3}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2)+\frac{3k_{3}^{2}}{2}.

(318)

For
\hat{i}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}^{3}}{u+v_{1}-v_{2}-v_{3}}\left[\frac{1}{3\epsilon}+\frac{2v_{1}}{3}(\log k_{1}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-\tfrac{7}{3})\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\frac{2v_{1}^{2}\epsilon}{3}\left((\log k_{1}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-\tfrac{7}{3})^{2}+\frac{19}{9}\right)\right],

\displaystyle i^{-+-}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=i^{+--}_{[333]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2},v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2}),

(320)

\displaystyle=i^{+--}_{[333]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{3},v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{3}),

(321)

\displaystyle i^{\text{fin}}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{1}^{3}+k_{2}^{3}+k_{3}^{3}}{3}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{4}{3})+\frac{1}{3}(\sigma_{(1)123}\sigma_{(2)123}-4\sigma_{(3)123}),

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{5(k_{1}^{3}+k_{2}^{3}+k_{3}^{3})+9k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}{27},

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[333]}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2}{3}k_{1}^{3}(\log k_{1}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-\tfrac{7}{3})(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{4}{3})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{3}k_{1}(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}-k_{2}k_{3})(\log k_{1}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-\tfrac{7}{3})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{3}\left[\sigma_{(1)123}\sigma_{(2)123}-2\sigma_{(3)123}-2k_{1}(k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2})\right](\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{4}{3})

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{k_{1}}{27}\left[20k_{1}^{2}+9k_{2}k_{3}+18k_{1}(k_{2}+k_{3})\right],

(324)

\displaystyle\hat{i}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[333]}

\displaystyle=\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[333]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2}),

(325)

\displaystyle=\hat{i}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[333]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{3}).

3-point amplitudes
\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}

The expansions of the amplitudes
\hat{j}_{[\Delta_{1}\Delta_{2};\Delta_{3}]}
to order
\epsilon^{1}
are as follows:

•

For
\hat{j}_{[22;2]}
:

\displaystyle j^{\pm\pm\pm}_{[22;2]}

\displaystyle=0,

(327)

\displaystyle j^{\text{fin}}_{[22;2]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right),

(328)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{u\epsilon}_{[22;2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E})^{2}-\frac{1}{4}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E})^{2},

(329)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[22;2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Big[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{23-}}{k_{t}}\right)-2\Li_{2}\left(-\frac{l_{13-}}{l_{12-}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E})^{2}-(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E})^{2}\Big],

(330)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[22;2]}

\displaystyle=\hat{j}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[22;2]}(k_{1}\leftrightarrow k_{2}),

(331)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{v_{3}\epsilon}_{[22;2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)-\log\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)(\log k_{t}-\gamma_{E}-2\log 2)\right]-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}.

(332)

•

For
\hat{j}_{[32;2]}

\displaystyle j^{--+}_{[32;2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{3}}{u-v_{1}-v_{2}+v_{3}}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}+2v_{3}(\log k_{3}-1)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+2v_{3}^{2}\epsilon\,(\log^{2}k_{3}-2\log k_{3}+2)\right],

(333)

\displaystyle j^{\text{fin}}_{[32;2]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{2}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E})+\frac{k_{2}-k_{3}}{2}(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E})+k_{3},

(334)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{u\epsilon}_{[32;2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{4}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}+\frac{k_{3}-k_{2}}{4}(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{k_{1}}{2}\log\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)+\frac{k_{3}}{2}\left(1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right),

(335)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{v_{1}\epsilon}_{[32;2]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{4}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{23-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{k_{2}-k_{3}}{4}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(-\frac{l_{13-}}{l_{12-}}\right)+(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{k_{1}}{2}\log\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)+\frac{k_{3}}{2}\left(-1+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right),

(336)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[32;2]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{2}-k_{3}}{4}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{13-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{4}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(-\frac{l_{23-}}{l_{12-}}\right)+(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\log\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)\left[-\frac{k_{1}}{2}+k_{2}(\log k_{2}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-1)\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{k_{3}}{2}\left(1-\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\right),

(337)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{v_{3}\epsilon}_{[32;2]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{k_{2}}{2}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{k_{2}+k_{3}}{2}(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-1)(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E}-1)

\displaystyle\qquad-k_{3}(\log k_{3}-1)(\log k_{t}+\log l_{12-}+2\gamma_{E}-2)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[\frac{k_{1}}{2}+k_{2}(\gamma_{E}+\log 2)\right]\log\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)-\frac{3}{2}\left(k_{3}-\frac{\pi^{2}}{18}(2k_{2}+k_{3})\right).

(338)

•

For
\hat{j}_{[22;3]}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle=\frac{-k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{4}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)-\log\left(\frac{l_{12-}}{k_{t}}\right)(\log k_{t}-\gamma_{E}-2\log 2-\tfrac{1}{2})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{-k_{1}k_{2}+k_{1}k_{3}+k_{2}k_{3}-k_{3}^{2}}{4}\log l_{12-}+\frac{k_{1}k_{2}+k_{1}k_{3}+k_{2}k_{3}+k_{3}^{2}}{4}\log k_{t}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{k_{3}(k_{1}+k_{2})}{4}(4\log k_{3}-2\gamma_{E}+1)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{12}(k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}-k_{3}^{2}).

For

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{8}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(-\frac{l_{13-}}{l_{12-}}\right)+(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{4}(k_{1}+k_{2})(2k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3})(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4}(k_{1}+k_{2})(2k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3})(\log l_{12-}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})

\displaystyle\qquad-k_{1}k_{3}(\log k_{1}+\gamma_{E}+\log 2-\tfrac{3}{2})-\frac{k_{2}k_{3}}{2},

(354)

\displaystyle\hat{j}^{v_{2}\epsilon}_{[32;3]}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{1}^{2}-k_{2}^{2}+k_{3}^{2}}{8}\left[2\Li_{2}\left(\frac{l_{13-}}{k_{t}}\right)+(\log k_{t}+\gamma_{E}-\tfrac{1}{2})^{2}\right]
